市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。
創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高： 9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元 ，再創單月新高；年增率高達 62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長 。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期 ，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季 。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳： 10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點 49,968點後面臨一波修正 ，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險 。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐 ，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前 ，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

3Q26 review: margin likely bottomed-out

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups   
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from relatedparties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

2
Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

Vanguard: Earnings estimate revisions
We raise our 2026 EPS forecast 11% but lower   2027 and 2028  EPS estimates 9% and

6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

Risk Reward - Vanguard International Semiconductor

Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: a cost of equity of 9.2%, a medium-term

Morgan Stanley EstimatesMean
Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$89.00 NT$250.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with
depreciation pressure during the early stage.

▪ Our UW rating is mostly because of the
margin pressure.

Risk Reward Themes
Disruption: Negative
Pricing Power: Positive
View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in
2026 and foundry growth is stagnant; and 2)
demand for 8" capacity is weaker than
expected owing to faster product migration.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
October Sales -6% MoM/+32%
YoY
Here we focus on AirTAC's monthly sales, which we believe
could be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• October sales came in at NT$3,565mn (-6% MoM/+32% YoY), mainly on 15%
fewer working days, as daily shipments were up 7% MoM.
• By segment, shipments of machine tools (+31% YoY), electronics (+30%
YoY), batteries (+27% YoY), machinery (+25% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY)

outperformed during the month.
• QTD revenue reached 33% of our estimate at NT$10,918mn (-5% QoQ/+21%
YoY) and 32% of consensus at NT$11,228mn (-2% QoQ/+24% YoY).
Outlook:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking in line with management guidance of
roughly flat QoQ – better than the typical seasonal decline.
• Management remains positive on the growth outlook, driven by share gains
alongside cyclical upturn tailwinds and government policy support.
• We remain OW, as the current valuation of 15x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs.

its mid-cycle average of 25x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

股價相對大盤走勢

長榮航太 (2645 TT)  Trading Buy

預期波音 737 系列交機速度將略為放緩。  
投資建議 ：雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量
波音將維持 737 系列月均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太
的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航太 Trading Buy 的投資建
議。  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造
的營運預估： 波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會
上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時
間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到 2H27 才會上升至 52
架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時間，故研
究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
3Q26 先前的維修漲價陸續貢獻，營運維持高檔： 3Q26 長榮航
太自 2H24 起陸續針對維修業務進行漲價後，加上全球機隊持
續供不應求，增加維修機率與維修產值，惟製造部門雖然波音
已將 737 系列交機上升至 47 架 /月，但短期拉貨力道仍有限，
使得長榮航太的製造業務與轉投資公司營運維持低檔，營運仍
以維修業務為主， 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元， QoQ+1.02% ，預估稅
後純益 8.31 億元， QoQ+10.34% ， EPS 2.22 元。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營
運新動能： 2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機
的逐年擴張之外，在新訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，
預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待後續取得特殊製程認證後，
2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單也有望成為長榮
航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ，EPS 10.04 元。

列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到
2H27 才會上升至 52 架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時
間，故研究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營運新動能：  
2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機的逐年擴張之外，在新
訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待
後續取得特殊製程認證後， 2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單
也有望成為長榮航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ， EPS 10.04 元。

均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航
太 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

### Page 7

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 500,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

주: 추정치에 반영된 판매량은 지역별 공장 판매 물량 기준으로, 실제 연결 기준 판매 물량(도매)과 차이 발생 
자료: 현대차, 대신증권 Research Center

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

현대차(005380)

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

- Overweight(비중확대)

  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

주가 추이

3분기 영업 이 익 은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일 회성 비용 등의 영 향 으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복 과 중 장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸 류 에이 션 에 주목합 니 다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

Document 276: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 27 日

1/5 
AI・半導体で韓国景気は堅調も、「Ｋ字型」の様相が一段と鮮明に 
～家計部門の低迷が李政権の重しとなる可能性～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 韓国景気は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要を追い風に、外需主導で堅調な推移が続いてい
る。 ７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比＋1.0％ （年率換算＋3.93％） 、 前年同期比＋3.4％
となり、2026 年９月までの累計成長率（＋3.6％）は中銀の８月時点の見通し（＋3.3％）を
上回った。メモリ半導体を中心とする財輸出に加え、半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連
の設備投資が成長を支え、2027 年も外需の堅調さが景気をけん引する展開が見込まれる。 
○ 一方、家計部門は力強さを欠いている。インフレ率は中銀目標の２％を上回って推移し、政
府の現金給付などの消費支援も効果が一巡したことから、個人消費は下振れた。成果給の恩
恵は一部の労働者にとどまり、業種間の賃金格差の拡大も懸念される。ソウルを中心に不動
産価格の上昇が続くなか、 家計債務を巡る金融リスクもくすぶっており、 中銀は10 月会合で
政策金利を据え置きつつも、追加利上げに含みを持たせた。KOSPI はピークから約４割下落
しており、金融市場の調整が家計に与える影響にも注意が必要である。 
○ 為替市場では、株価調整とは対照的に、ウォン相場が底堅く推移している。過去最高を更新
した貿易黒字に伴う実需と、中銀のタカ派姿勢が下支えとなっている。 
○ 政治面では、李在明政権の支持率が過去最低に近い水準にある。企業と家計、資産保有層と
それ以外の層との間で景気回復の恩恵に格差が生じる「Ｋ字型」の様相が強まっており、金
融市場の混乱が家計部門に波及すれば、政権の求心力が一段と低下する可能性がある。

【世界的なAI・半導体需要の旺盛さを追い風に、足元の韓国景気は堅調な推移が続いている】 
韓国では、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛な動きが景気の追い風となる展開が
続いている（注１）。韓国は、DRAM やNAND 型フラッシュメモリ、広帯域メモリ（ＨＢＭ）などメモ
リ半導体の大規模量産で世界トップクラスの競争力を有する。旺盛な半導体需要をけん引役に９月
の輸出額は過去最高額を更新するなど、輸出の勢いは強まっている。韓国はＧＤＰに占める輸出の

注１ 10 月１日付レポート「韓国､AI・半導体需要が景気に追い風も､政治への逆風は止まず」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666170.html）

### Page 2

2/5 
比率が４割を上回るなど、 アジア太平洋
地域のなかでも外需依存度が相対的に
高い。こうしたなか、 ７-９月の実質ＧＤ
Ｐ成長率は前期比＋1.0％、年率換算ベ
ースでは＋3.93％となり、 景気の堅調さ
が確認されている （図１）。 中期的な基調
を示す前年同期比ベースの成長率も＋
3.4％と前期（同＋3.7％）から伸びが鈍
化しているものの、 ３四半期連続で３％
を上回る水準で推移している。 
世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大
を反映して財輸出が押し上げられるとともに（図２）、中国人観光客を中心とする来訪者数の増加
はサービス輸出を押し上げるなど、堅調な外需が景気をけん引する動きがみられる。さらに、 ＡＩ ・
半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に、 半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資が拡大するなど、
設備投資も景気を下支えしている。ま
た、政府消費の増加も成長に寄与した。
一方で、政府はイラン情勢の悪化を受け
た原油高の影響軽減を目的に、低所得者
層などを対象に１人当たり 10 万～60 万
ウォンの現金給付を実施したほか、 省エ
ネ家電の購入促進策 （キャッシュバック
の実施）などの消費支援に動いた。しか
し、足元ではその効果が一巡しており、
個人消費は下振れしている。さらに、前
期は在庫調整が進む動きが確認された
ものの、 当期は前期比年率ベースで＋0.32pt と試算されるなど、在庫の積み上がりを示唆する動き
もうかがえる。 
とはいえ、2026 年の９月までの累計ベースの経済成長率は＋3.6％となり、中銀が８月時点で示
した見通し （＋3.3％）を上回っている。 中銀は10 月会合において、2026 年の経済成長率が８月時
点の見通しを大きく上回る可能性を示唆したが、 足元の状況はそうした見方に沿った動きと捉えら
れる。 さらに、2027 年についても、 世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の動向に留意する必要があるも
のの、外需の堅調さが景気をけん引する展開が続く可能性が見込まれる。

### Page 3

3/5 
【家計部門はインフレに直面、賃金上昇の恩恵は一部にとどまり、不動産高騰や株価調整も重し】 
外需は堅調な動きをみせる一方、対照的に個人消費は力強さを欠いている。背景には、2025 年後
半以降のインフレ率が中銀目標（２％）を上回る伸びで推移するなど、実質購買力に下押し圧力が
かかっていることがある。さらに、イラ
ン情勢の悪化を受けた 原油高を背景に
物価上昇圧力が残り、インフレ率は中銀
目標を上回る水準で推移している （図
３） 。これを受けて、政府は燃料価格の上
限規制 を約６ヵ月維持するなど物価対
策に動いている。しかし、足元では中東
情勢 の不透明 感を理由に 原油価格は高
止まりするなど、見通しが立たない状況
が続いている。一方で、半導体産業など
一部企業では、営業利益などの一定割合
（Ｎ％）を従業員の成果報酬の原資とする「Ｎ％成果給」が導入され、他産業にも導入を求める動
きが広がっている。 とはいえ、実際に高額な成果報酬の恩恵を受ける労働者は一部にとどまるなど、
業種間の賃金格差の拡大に繋がる懸念も高まっている。 
韓国ではここ数年、首都ソウルを中心とする不動産価格の高騰が社会問題化してきた。 政府は
2025 年以降に 様々な不動産高騰対策を
打ち出したものの、一連の対策では実需
層の資金調達が困難になる一方、規制対
象外の地域で不動産価格が上昇するな
ど「副作用」も顕在化した。また、政府
は超高額住宅や所有者が居住していな
い住宅に対する課税強化を目指したも
のの、中高所得者層などからの反発を受
けて一部見直しを余儀なくされた。さら
に、一連の政府主導による不動産対策に
対する不満は、６月に実施されたソウル
市長選で与党候補が敗北した一因との見方もある。とはいえ、 足元においてもソウルを中心とする
不動産価格は上昇が続いており （図４） 、住宅関連融資の増加が続くなど、 家計債務を巡る金融リス
クもくすぶっている。中銀は、10 月の定例会合で３会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置いたものの、 追加
利上げに含みを持たせる考えを示した（注２）。背景のひとつには、 不動産市場を巡るリスクがある

注２ 10 月22 日付レポート「韓国中銀、株価急落などで利上げ休止も、金融引き締めの長期化を示唆」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666810.html）

### Page 4

4/5 
と考えられる。 
2025 年以降の韓国金融市場では、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の旺盛な動きを追い風に、主要
株価指数（KOSPI）は上昇基調を強めた。
さらに、 政府は2025 年末、 個人投資家が
保有する海外株式を売却して国内株式
に１年間投資した場合、一定の条件の下
で譲渡所得税を最大 100％控除する時限
措置を発表し、2026 年３月に法制化し
た。そのうえ、株価上昇を受けて個人投
資家の間でレバレッジ投資が活発化し
たことも、株価上昇の動きを後押しする
一助となった。しかし、構成銘柄全体の
時価総額に占める割合が５割前後を占
めるサムスン電子とＳＫハイニックスの２社の株価動向にKOSPI が左右されやすい構造にある。 こ
うしたなか、足元ではピークから約４割下落するなど、市場は大幅な調整に直面している（図５）
（注３）。こうした金融市場の不安定な動きも、家計部門に悪影響を与えている可能性がある。

【株価の大幅調整が続いているものの、貿易黒字と中銀のタカ派姿勢がウォン相場を下支え】 
金融市場においては、前述の通り半導体関連株を中心とする株価調整の動きに直面しているもの
の、 通貨ウォン相場は対照的に底堅い動きをみせている （図６） 。 背景には、世界的なＡＩ・半導体
関連需要の旺盛さが輸出を押し上げる
なか、足元の貿易黒字は過去最高を更新
するなど、ウォンに対する実需が生まれ
やすい環境にあることが挙げられる。ま
た、中銀が断続的な利上げを実施すると
ともに、先行きも追加利上げに含みを持
たせるなど「タカ派」姿勢を強めている
ことがある。さらに、 中銀の申総裁は10
月の定例会合後に行った記者会見にお
いて、「足元のウォンの対ドル相場は歴
史的に見れば低水準」 、 「実質実効ベース
でもウォンは依然割安」、「ウォン高は経済的不平等を是正する効果がある」と述べるなど、ウォン
高の経済的な利点を指摘したことも、ウォン相場を後押ししている可能性がある。

注３ 10 月15 日付レポート「韓国株が急落、AI・半導体好況の陰で市場の調整が深まる」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666600.html）

### Page 5

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
5/5
【金融市場の混乱や家計部門を取り巻く環境が李在明政権の行方を左右する可能性に要注意】
こうしたなか、李在明（イ・ジェミョン）政権の支持率は低水準で推移しており、足元では過去
最低に近い水準で推移している。 このところの支持率低下を巡っては、 若年層などで支持が弱まる
動きがみられるほか、 不動産政策や物価高への不満も影響していると考えられる。 こうした動きは、
政権が支持層をつなぎとめることの難しさを示唆している。足元の景気はいわゆる「Ｋ字型」の様
相を強めるとともに、 企業と家計、 資産保有層とそれ以外の層との間で景気回復の恩恵に格差が生
じており、その格差が一段と拡大する兆しもみられる。このため、先行きは実体経済の堅調さにも

かかわらず、李政権を取り巻く環境が厳しさを増す可能性も考えられる。与党・共に民主党を巡っ
ても、党内では政策運営などを巡る意見対立がみられるなど、火種を抱えている。金融市場の混乱
が家計部門に波及することで、 李政権の求心力をさらに弱める可能性がある点には注意が必要であ
る。
以 上

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 347: European bank M&A is back with a bang

Source PDF: all report pdf/European bank M&A is back with a bang.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and
2027 should stay busy. Domestic deals still dominate, while
cross-border consolidation is harder but is also picking up.
Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and
execution still matter. Expect more deals, but a transformation
of Europe's banking sector is still some way oﬀ
Jesse Norcross
UniCredit and Commerzbank are at the centre of Europe’s closely watched
push towards cross-border banking consolidation

European bank M&A is back
European bank M&A has seen a strong resurgence, with several
large-scale deals making headlines. UniCredit’s high-stakes
takeover of Commerzbank, once deemed very unlikely but now
moving closer to completion, could be Europe’s most signiﬁcant
cross-border transaction in years. Italy remains one of Europe’s
bank M&A hotbeds, with multiple banks taking part in the ongoing
consolidation wave. Other banks have also been active, and we

expect M&A to remain elevated in 2027.

### Page 2

Year-to-date M&A deal value has already surpassed the 2025 total,
reaching €53bn including the UniCredit-Commerzbank transaction
and based on deals valued at €100m or more. Furthermore, deals
pending or intended total €39bn, which includes the potential
takeover of Banca Monte dei Paschi by Intesa Sanpaolo, currently
valued at around €34bn. All in, this would bring deal value back to
levels not seen since the early 2010s.
European bank M&A volumes have seen a

resurgence
(€bn)

Source: Reﬁnitiv, ING
The rationale for European bank M&A has changed. After the
ﬁnancial crisis, deals were mostly about repair, followed by a wave
of deals involving stronger domestic banks buying weaker rivals.
Today, the backdrop has shifted – proﬁtability and capital levels are
stronger, making the obvious cost-eﬃciency story less compelling

on its own. Instead, banks are using M&A to add scale, deposits and
fee-generating diversiﬁcation, while the need to close the gap with
US rivals remains a key driver.
The industrial logic has also strengthened, as ﬁxed costs are rising
in areas such as technology, AI, compliance, as well as personnel.
Rising investment requirements mean that remaining subscale is
becoming more expensive, even for highly proﬁtable banks.

### Page 3

Domestic dominates, but cross-border
competes
UniCredit-Commerzbank remains the sector’s most closely
watched deal and could become the ﬁrst major cross-border
acquisition since the ﬁnancial crisis. UniCredit has slowly been
building its stake rather than a clean takeover and is now deemed
to hold eﬀective control, despite currently owning around 50% of
the shares. Furthermore, it is also not entirely a leap into an
unfamiliar market, as UniCredit already has a signiﬁcant banking

business in Germany through its subsidiary, HVB.
The deal has also faced considerable pushback as well as political
and stakeholder opposition, but is now looking increasingly likely to
proceed. It has highlighted the diﬃculty of pulling oﬀ a large-scale
cross-border deal for European banks. Yet it is now becoming the
clearest live test of whether Europe is genuinely ready for cross-
border bank consolidation.
Domestic deals remain the rule, but we have seen other examples

of cross-border deals. These include BPCE’s acquisition of
Portuguese peer Novo Banco; Erste Bank's acquisition of Santander
Bank Polska; and Crédit Agricole acquiring IKB to increase its
presence in Germany. Some banks are also looking across the
pond, such as Santander, which acquired Webster Financial
Corporation in the US for USD12.5bn.
These transactions are important, but they do not yet amount to a
broad-based cross-border consolidation wave. Many involve banks

already familiar with the target market or assets that complement
an existing local business.

Italy is still riding the wave
Italy has become one of Europe’s busiest M&A markets, riding a
new wave of consolidation, with Banca Monte dei Paschi (MPS) at
the epicentre. The bank completed the acquisition of Mediobanca in
2025, and this year approached Banco BPM and Banca Generali for
a merger as a counteroﬀensive to Intesa Sanpaolo’s hostile bid for
MPS.
The counteroﬀensive now appears less likely, as key shareholders

have opposed parts of MPS’s merger plans and have also backed
Intesa’s bid. The ECB has also provided authorisation to take control
of MPS, representing an important step for the €34bn takeover.
Alongside this, we had the BPER–Popolare di Sondrio merger earlier
in the year, adding to Italy’s consolidation.
Why is Italy so active in particular? Well, Italy remains quite
fragmented (see below) and still oﬀers a large domestic synergy
pool, even after the wave of M&A that has already taken place.

Furthermore, the country has meaningful industrial scale and oﬀers
further revenue diversiﬁcation opportunities for banks and insurers.
Although the obvious combinations are becoming less evident in
Italy, we expect this to continue to drive M&A activity in 2027.

country (2025)

It's not only about getting bigger
The deal mix is also broadening, as banks are increasingly focusing
on fee income as well as scale, while also stepping into capital-light
businesses. Deals in sectors such as wealth management, asset
management, payments & FX and insurance are likely to feature
prominently.
NatWest Group’s acquisition of Evelyn Partners is a good example.

The combination created the UK’s largest Private Banking and
Wealth Management business, while lifting Natwest’s fee income
by 20%. Similarly, BNP Paribas’s acquisition of AXA Investment
Managers in 2025 created a €1.5tn asset management business,
as the bank is seeking to compete with US asset management
rivals and grow its fee income. In Germany, Deutsche Bank and
DWS were reported to be exploring a potential minority investment
in the life insurance business Frankfurter Leben. This would mark

Deutsche’s return to life insurance after more than a decade.
Consolidation can support bank
performance
But does consolidation actually deliver better outcomes? The
evidence is not entirely clear-cut, but overall points in the right
direction. While increasing scale is not always the best use of
capital, in general, more consolidated markets tend to have more
stable and, in some cases, higher proﬁtability. The ECB is in favour
of further consolidation where appropriate, as it sees the beneﬁts of

diversiﬁcation and eﬃciency. On the other hand, capital can be
reduced following a merger. This could be a downside, as better
capitalised banks tend to enjoy lower funding costs and thus have
more room to lend to the real economy.
The outcome therefore depends on the trade-oﬀ between longer-
term synergies and the upfront capital, execution and integration
costs.
ECB data show an association between more concentrated banking
markets and proﬁtability. As the chart below shows, countries with

a high level of concentration (deﬁned by a higher Herﬁndahl-
Hirschman Index coeﬃcient) tend to have higher net interest
margins on average. Still, net interest margins in Europe are
structurally lower than in the US, where they tend to be between 3–
4%.

Similarly, a higher level of market concentration in European
countries is associated with a lower cost of risk. The correlation is
modest, with some clear outliers, but the numbers show that
greater concentration can lead to lower asset quality costs,
although the relationship should perhaps not be interpreted as
causal. Still, taken together, this suggests that consolidation can
improve banking markets.

Regulation is helping, but can get in the way
The Banking Union has made deals easier to assess: the ECB has
become the single prudential supervisor for large banks, capital
rules are more consistent, while resolution planning is now much

clearer than it was even a decade ago. But as the Draghi report
noted, the incomplete Banking Union means Europe’s banks remain
less proﬁtable than their US counterparts.
There is a shift underway, though. The EU Commission plans to
table a broad Banking Package in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, with
measures covering cross-border capital and liquidity management,
deposit insurance, prudential rules and other items. For M&A, the
most important proposals include those to give banking groups

more ﬂexibility to allocate capital and liquidity more eﬃciently
within cross-border groups, while also applying more consistent

treatment to intra-group exposures. These remain proposals rather
than agreed implementations, meaning their ﬁnal scope and
timetable remain uncertain.
The AFME estimates that regulatory ring-fencing rules mean that
some €225bn of capital and €250bn of liquidity is trapped within
subsidiaries, which banks cannot freely move between parent and
subsidiaries. Freeing this up could also support more cross-border
transactions.

The European Commission is also bringing forward a new proposal
to replace the European Deposit Insurance Scheme (EDIS), which
could reduce national ring-fencing incentives and encourage more
cross-border M&A. CMDI reform has been passed this year, which
could make it easier to deal with failing banks, potentially making
consolidation easier as well, although the direct impact on M&A is

unclear.
The Danish compromise, which is now embedded into CRR3, allows
banks to risk-weight certain insurance subsidiaries rather than
deduct them from regulatory capital. This was clariﬁed by the EBA
in January 2026, and some banks such as UniCredit have received
authorisation to use the Danish Compromise methodology for its
insurance subsidiaries. This has improved the economics of

### Page 9

bancassurance and could support further bank-insurance
transactions.
On the other hand, national regulatory and political intervention
has also put the brakes on M&A in some cases, raising execution
costs and uncertainty. BBVA’s proposed acquisition of Sabadell in
2024 illustrates the problem. It took 539 days from the
announcement in May 2024 to the oﬀer failing in October 2025.
For us, the message seems clear that European regulators are
seeking to improve bank competitiveness, and they see

consolidation as an important part of this. Looking ahead,
regulation can improve the plumbing, but it is not a dealmaker.
Why 2027 should stay busy
The starting point for 2027 remains supportive, with bank
proﬁtability continuing to hold up well in 2026 while capital levels
remain solid. While the geopolitical, inﬂation and interest rate
backdrop poses headwinds, we still expect acquisition activity to
remain high. On the other hand, management teams will

increasingly need to choose between retaining greater capital
headroom in a less market-friendly environment, shareholder
payouts, or M&A.
The most likely deals are still domestic. Italy remains the M&A
market of choice, but other geographies could also stand out. The
Nordics are likely to see more partnerships and tech- and wealth-
focused deals. Spain and Portugal are already quite concentrated,
so we could see more bolt-ons. In the UK, challenger banks and

neobanks are gaining ground while deposit competition remains
intense, potentially leading to more activity. The Benelux has
potential catalysts following a series of government ownership
reductions and increasing inorganic ambitions.
Cross-border activity could also remain elevated, but we think the
real litmus test remains the UniCredit-Commerzbank deal, including
what happens after completion in terms of execution and
integration, a process that is likely to take a few years. The

regulatory environment should also become increasingly more

supportive for cross-border M&A from 2027 onwards.
Overall, 2027 could be another interesting year.
M&A is here to stay
European bank M&A has picked up in recent years, and we expect
more activity again in 2027, although we do not see a big bang. For
investors, positioning is inherently speculative. For bondholders, the
main way to gain is by owning the target entity's bonds. However,
this is not always the case, and bigger doesn’t always mean better,

as credit ratings don’t necessarily improve either following an
acquisition or merger.
We are not suggesting that a major change in the European
banking landscape is imminent, as many obstacles remain,
particularly with cross-border deals. But the direction of travel is
interesting: larger banks often outperform smaller banks, while
regulators continue to see beneﬁts from a deeper, more integrated
European banking union. Still, European banking markets remain

fragmented to diﬀering degrees, both between countries and
compared to the US, presenting both opportunities and obstacles.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.
